# Coverage table

Every requirement in the Master Specification and the five scene sub-specs, linked to a result or an explicit exception (Production Plan M7 exit evidence). Audited 2026-09-30 (checkpoint 4 close-out: every pending row re-verified after the fixes, captures and measurements, each upgrade checked by an independent skeptic) at commit `7908069`; regenerate with `node --experimental-strip-types scripts/coverage-table.ts` from `docs/coverage.json`.

- **met**: implemented and checked (test, measurement or recorded review).
- **exception**: an approved or recorded deviation (see `docs/spec-revisions.md`).
- **partial**: implemented, but part of the evidence is missing.
- **unverified**: needs a device, a person or a tool this production does not have (mostly the phone session, VoiceOver/NVDA and other browsers).
- **not met**: open.

| Master Specification | 6 Motion: primary | Guest-ready entrance at most 250 ms, skipped when loading late, never delaying controls | partial | The ≤250 ms length and the late-load skip rest only on reading the constants. A search of tests/, docs/ and scripts/ finds no test, capture or measurement of the entrance; scripts/capture.ts:451 only waits for .is-visible. business.spec.ts:203 holds the GLB past 6 s but never asserts that the transition was skipped. A browser assertion on the computed canvas transition, for both a normal and a held load, would close this. (skeptic: 'met' requires implementation plus a check. Only the 'never delaying controls' part is checked; the duration and late-skip behaviour have no test, measurement or recorded review.) |
| Master Specification | 6 Motion: selective physics | Pointer cancellation returns to the chosen authored path | partial | tests/browser/resilience.spec.ts:75 does not test a cancelled drag. It dispatches synthetic touch events with pointerId 7. controller.ts:548 calls setPointerCapture before p.begin() at :549, and Chrome throws NotFoundError for a pointer id that is not active. So begin() never runs, pointermove is ignored (dragging false), and pointercancel ends a drag that never started. The test passes whatever the cancel path does. I reproduced the throw in a standalone headless Chrome probe (scratchpad/master2-skeptic/capture-probe.mjs, no dev server): the synthetic touch threw, the real mouse captured. The cancel wiring is therefore covered only by code reading, plus the sibling mouse-release test. A fix would use a real touch or mouse drag (page.touchscreen, or CDP Input.dispatchTouchEvent with a cancel), or would call begin() before setPointerCapture. (skeptic: The audit said the dedicated pointercancel test closes the gap. It does not exercise a drag, so no check of the cancellation path exists.) |
| Master Specification | 6 Motion: interruption | Blend through the canonical ready pose over at most 150 ms, using already loaded assets | partial | The blend meets the spec, and the Birthday, Dinner and Sports-at-rim cuts fall under #9. Two behaviours are neither fixed nor covered by #9. (1) Business settle() (src/scenes/business/index.ts:132-137) still zeroes the card sway spring in one frame before the blend capture, a 0 ms pop of up to about 1.65°; card_secondary is not in animatedNodes (:68). motion-interrupt-blend-quality was deferred (qualification.md:145, 239), but that deferral names only the eased/cross-dissolve blend and the Party label, not this snap. (2) Sports also cuts while the net is still settling, up to 0.4 s after the ball leaves it, which #9's wording does not list. Both are polish-level. No further fix is pending. |

| Birthday — Cake | Inherited master rules (Birthday instance) | Change response blends through the canonical ready pose in ≤150 ms (Master §6) | partial | Accept to decline blends linearly over 150 ms, which meets Master §6. The flames regrow during the blend, and its verifier judged that conforming. Decline to accept after the cake has left the stand cuts to ready, and row 9 covers that as far as the cake-from-bin case goes. But the cut also fires for the whole first 0.25 s of the decline, while the cake is still on the stand and only the bin is sliding in. A straight blend there would move the bin back along its own entry line and relight the flames, passing nothing through anything. That cut is therefore outside row 9's stated condition and outside interruptCut's own docstring. It is the same kind of gap that kept Sports at partial (motion-sports-cut-midflight) until its fix. What the viewer sees is a one-frame jump: the bin vanishes and the flames return at full size. The likely cause: capture() stores only position, rotation and scale, so the director cannot blend the bin's visibility. There are two ways to close this: narrow interruptCut to 'cake off the stand' and handle bin visibility in the blend, or widen row 9 to record the early-decline cut. I did not check the carry window (0.25-0.72 s). No checkpoint-4 fix covers this. The eased or cross-dissolved blend remains deferred polish (qualification.md:145, :239). visualDirector.ts is unchanged since c1839e8. controller.ts did change in the Engine merge 66bafde, but not in the blend path, so the old 'src/core unchanged' claim no longer holds as written. (skeptic: Downgrade: the recorded exception (row 9) covers only part of the behaviour. The scene cuts in 0-0.25 s of the decline, where by row 9's own condition a straight blend would be clean, so 'exception' does not cover the row as a whole.) |

| 6 Motion: selective physics | Pointer cancellation returns to the chosen authored path | partial | src/core/controller.ts:569-592 (pointercancel and lostpointercapture go to onPointerUp, then endPointer(true), which releases capture and calls scene.pointer.end); src/scenes/business/index.ts:282 (end() ignores the cancelled flag and calls release()), :298-315 (release rebases onto the scanner approach and blends back over 0.45 s with the timeline held); tests/browser/business.spec.ts:89 exercises the same release() through a real mouse pointerup (the drag holds the timeline, the scan completes, 1 request) | tests/browser/resilience.spec.ts:75 does not test a cancelled drag. It dispatches synthetic touch events with pointerId 7. controller.ts:548 calls setPointerCapture before p.begin() at :549, and Chrome throws NotFoundError for a pointer id that is not active. So begin() never runs, pointermove is ignored (dragging false), and pointercancel ends a drag that never started. The test passes whatever the cancel path does. I reproduced the throw in a standalone headless Chrome probe (scratchpad/master2-skeptic/capture-probe.mjs, no dev server): the synthetic touch threw, the real mouse captured. The cancel wiring is therefore covered only by code reading, plus the sibling mouse-release test. A fix would use a real touch or mouse drag (page.touchscreen, or CDP Input.dispatchTouchEvent with a cancel), or would call begin() before setPointerCapture. (skeptic: The audit said the dedicated pointercancel test closes the gap. It does not exercise a drag, so no check of the cancellation path exists.) |

High is never selected automatically and is reachable only through setQualityTier or the Developer drawer, and desktop is not a reference device. On phone-sized layouts, High stays at 37–41 MiB. (2) The drawing buffer costs about 36 bytes per pixel with MSAA. So at Normal, Party passes 48 MiB once the stage is larger than about 560 CSS px at DPR 1.5 or more. First, the framebuffer figure is for a 534² buffer, and a large desktop stage can grow the drawing buffer up to the 2.5 MP cap (createRenderer.ts:4); that is framebuffer memory, not texture allocation. Second, qualification.md:296 still says Low drops the gobo, while style-reference.md:20 says Low keeps a 192² copy. (skeptic: Correction: the note described the PMREM depth-buffer saving as pending in an unmerged batch, but it is merged and tested at HEAD. Status unchanged.) |

A Birthday lose/restore at HEAD (scratchpad/bdm7sk/restore2.mjs) returns to ready with one canvas and zero console warnings or errors, and Guest → Accept then saves and ends on accepted-pose. src/demo/main.ts:355-358 sets the notice copy by mode and by poster or text fallback (eae932b). | Inherited master rules (Birthday instance) | Change response blends through the canonical ready pose in ≤150 ms (Master §6) | partial | src/core/visualDirector.ts:141-157: a straight blend through guest-ready over INTERRUPT_BLEND 0.15 s, with a linear k at :190. When the scene's interruptCut() is true, the director poses guest-ready at once. src/scenes/birthday/index.ts:55-59: interruptCut() returns f.binVisible \|\| !f.cakeVisible \|\| the cake is off the stand. Its docstring reads 'From the bin (or on the way), a straight blend back would pull the cake through the stand'. src/scenes/birthday/sequences.ts:108-122 (decline): binVisible = t > 0; the bin slides on a straight line from BIN_FROM (-3.8, 0, -1.72) to BIN_AT (-1.04, 0, -1.72) over 0-0.24 s; the cake stays exactly ON_STAND until t = 0.25. I sampled the decline at HEAD (scratchpad/bdm7sk/cut.mts, run with node --experimental-transform-types). interruptCut is true from t = 0.05 s while the cake is still on the stand and the bin is still sliding in (binX -3.48 at 0.05 s, -1.04 at 0.25 s). docs/spec-revisions.md row 9 records a 0 ms cut only 'where a straight blend would pass objects through each other (cake from the bin, ...)'. tests/visual-director.test.ts:49 (cut) and :70 (blend) pass; the unit suite is 134/134 at HEAD 9866254, whose src/ matches 3f215c8. scratchpad/cp4/technical/phaseC-birthday.json: decline→accept at f 0.05 (75 ms) and every other case end on the exact canonical still, but it checks final stills only, not the transition. outputs/birthday/change.mp4 (15:52) shows only accept→decline at 0.5 s, the direction that blends. | Accept to decline blends linearly over 150 ms, which meets Master §6. The flames regrow during the blend, and its verifier judged that conforming. Decline to accept after the cake has left the stand cuts to ready, and row 9 covers that as far as the cake-from-bin case goes. But the cut also fires for the whole first 0.25 s of the decline, while the cake is still on the stand and only the bin is sliding in. A straight blend there would move the bin back along its own entry line and relight the flames, passing nothing through anything. That cut is therefore outside row 9's stated condition and outside interruptCut's own docstring. It is the same kind of gap that kept Sports at partial (motion-sports-cut-midflight) until its fix. What the viewer sees is a one-frame jump: the bin vanishes and the flames return at full size. The likely cause: capture() stores only position, rotation and scale, so the director cannot blend the bin's visibility. There are two ways to close this: narrow interruptCut to 'cake off the stand' and handle bin visibility in the blend, or widen row 9 to record the early-decline cut. I did not check the carry window (0.25-0.72 s). No checkpoint-4 fix covers this. The eased or cross-dissolved blend remains deferred polish (qualification.md:145, :239). visualDirector.ts is unchanged since c1839e8. controller.ts did change in the Engine merge 66bafde, but not in the blend path, so the old 'src/core unchanged' claim no longer holds as written. (skeptic: Downgrade: the recorded exception (row 9) covers only part of the behaviour. The scene cuts in 0-0.25 s of the decline, where by row 9's own condition a straight blend would be clean, so 'exception' does not cover the row as a whole.) |

The 0 ms cuts in the collision window are the deviation recorded in docs/spec-revisions.md #9. The net-only window (1.09–1.50 s, about 0.41 s of the 1.7 s made shot) is not covered by #9's wording, which names only objects passing through each other. The code's reason for it ('a blend would snap the swinging net to rest') does not separate the two paths. The cut also puts the net at rest at once: SportsScene.apply via frameFor('guest-ready') sets flare 0, just as refresh() does at the start of a blend (net.update(0, 0)). So this window's cut is a design choice: one clean discontinuity, rather than a gliding ball next to a net that has snapped. The register needs this trigger added to #9 at reconciliation, not just 'could say this'. An eased or cross-dissolved blend is deferred polish (qualification.md 'Deferred').
